\displaystyle=\overline{F}(h_{\Gamma}^{01})\left(\overline{F}(h_{\Gamma_{1}}^{12})\tau_{f_{2}}^{A}\circ\left(\overline{F}(h_{\Gamma_{1}})\circ\overline{F}(\of_{2})\right)\tau_{\rho^{\prime}}^{A}\circ\left(\phi_{h_{\Gamma_{1}},f_{2}}^{\overline{F}(\overline{\rho}^{\prime})A}\right)^{-1}\circ\phi_{f_{1},h_{\Gamma_{1}^{\prime}}}^{\overline{F}(\overline{\rho}^{\prime})A}\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\overline{F}(h_{\Gamma}^{01})\circ\overline{F}(h_{\Gamma_{1}}^{12})\right)\left(\tau_{f_{2}}^{A}\circ\overline{F}(\of_{2})\tau_{\rho^{\prime}}^{A}\right)\circ\overline{F}(h_{\Gamma}^{01})\left(\left(\phi_{h_{\Gamma_{1}},f_{2}}^{\overline{F}(\overline{\rho}^{\prime})A}\right)^{-1}\circ\phi_{f_{1},h_{\Gamma_{1}^{\prime}}}^{\overline{F}(\overline{\rho}^{\prime})A}\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\overline{F}(h_{\Gamma}^{01})\circ\overline{F}(h_{\Gamma_{1}}^{12})\right)\left(\tau_{\rho^{\prime}\circ f_{2}}^{A}\circ\phi_{f_{2},\rho^{\prime}}^{A}\right)\circ\overline{F}(h_{\Gamma}^{01})\left(\left(\phi_{h_{\Gamma_{1}},f_{2}}^{\overline{F}(\overline{\rho}^{\prime})A}\right)^{-1}\circ\phi_{f_{1},h_{\Gamma_{1}^{\prime}}}^{\overline{F}(\overline{\rho}^{\prime})A}\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\overline{F}(h_{\Gamma}^{01})\circ\overline{F}(h_{\Gamma_{1}}^{12})\right)\left(\tau_{f_{3}\circ\rho}^{A}\circ\phi_{f_{2},\rho^{\prime}}^{A}\right)\circ\overline{F}(h_{\Gamma}^{01})\left(\left(\phi_{h_{\Gamma_{1}},f_{2}}^{\overline{F}(\overline{\rho}^{\prime})A}\right)^{-1}\circ\phi_{f_{1},h_{\Gamma_{1}^{\prime}}}^{\overline{F}(\overline{\rho}^{\prime})A}\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\overline{F}(h_{\Gamma}^{01})\circ\overline{F}(h_{\Gamma_{1}}^{12})\right)\left(\tau_{\rho}^{A}\circ\overline{F}(\rho)\tau_{f_{3}}^{A}\circ\left(\phi_{\rho,f_{3}}^{A}\right)^{-1}\circ\phi_{f_{2},\rho^{\prime}}^{A}\right)

\displaystyle\circ\overline{F}(h_{\Gamma}^{01})\left(\left(\phi_{h_{\Gamma_{1}},f_{2}}^{\overline{F}(\overline{\rho}^{\prime})A}\right)^{-1}\circ\phi_{f_{1},h_{\Gamma_{1}^{\prime}}}^{\overline{F}(\overline{\rho}^{\prime})A}\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\overline{F}(h_{\Gamma}^{01})\circ\overline{F}(h_{\Gamma_{1}}^{12})\right)\tau_{\rho}^{A}\circ\left(\overline{F}(h_{\Gamma}^{01})\circ\overline{F}(h_{\Gamma_{1}}^{12})\right)\left(\overline{F}(\rho)\tau_{f_{3}}^{A}\circ\left(\phi_{\rho,f_{3}}^{A}\right)^{-1}\circ\phi_{f_{2},\rho^{\prime}}^{A}\right)

\displaystyle\circ\overline{F}(h_{\Gamma}^{01})\left(\left(\phi_{h_{\Gamma_{1}},f_{2}}^{\overline{F}(\overline{\rho}^{\prime})A}\right)^{-1}\circ\phi_{f_{1},h_{\Gamma_{1}^{\prime}}}^{\overline{F}(\overline{\rho}^{\prime})A}\right).

Computing that

\,{}_{\Gamma}{\alpha_{A}}\circ\tau_{f}^{(\psi\circ\varphi)^{\sharp}A}

\displaystyle=\left(\overline{F}(h_{\Gamma}^{01})\circ\overline{F}(h_{\Gamma_{1}}^{12})\right)\tau_{\rho}^{A}\circ\left(\phi_{h^{01}_{\Gamma},h_{\Gamma_{1}}}^{\overline{F}(\overline{\rho})A}\right)^{-1}\circ\left(\phi_{h^{12}_{\Gamma_{1}}\circ h^{01}_{\Gamma},\rho}\right)^{-1}\circ\overline{F}(h_{\Gamma}^{02})\tau_{f_{3}}^{A}

\displaystyle\circ\left(\phi_{h_{\Gamma}^{02},f_{3}}^{A}\right)^{-1}\circ\phi_{f,h_{\Gamma^{\prime}}^{02}}^{A}

and realizing that by combining our derivations above we have calculated and manipulated the morphism
\tau_{f}^{\varphi^{\sharp}(\psi^{\sharp}A)}\circ\overline{F}(\of)\,_{\Gamma^{\prime}}{\alpha_{A}}
, to prove that the two composites coincide it suffices to verify that the morphism

\displaystyle\left(\overline{F}(h_{\Gamma}^{01})\circ\overline{F}(h_{\Gamma_{1}}^{12})\right)\left(\overline{F}(\rho)\tau_{f_{3}}^{A}\circ\left(\phi_{\rho,f_{3}}^{A}\right)^{-1}\circ\phi_{f_{2},\rho^{\prime}}^{A}\right)\circ\overline{F}(h_{\Gamma}^{01})\left(\left(\phi_{h_{\Gamma_{1}},f_{2}}^{\overline{F}(\overline{\rho}^{\prime})A}\right)^{-1}\circ\phi_{f_{1},h_{\Gamma_{1}^{\prime}}}^{\overline{F}(\overline{\rho}^{\prime})A}\right)

\displaystyle\circ\overline{F}(\of)\left(\left(\phi_{h_{\Gamma^{\prime}},h_{\Gamma^{\prime}_{1}}}^{\overline{F}(\overline{\rho}^{\prime})A}\right)^{-1}\circ\left(\phi_{h_{\Gamma_{1}^{\prime}}\circ h_{\Gamma^{\prime}},\rho^{\prime}}^{A}\right)^{-1}\right)

is equal to the morphism

\left(\phi_{h^{01}_{\Gamma},h_{\Gamma_{1}}}^{\overline{F}(\overline{\rho})A}\right)^{-1}\circ\left(\phi_{h^{12}_{\Gamma_{1}}\circ h^{01}_{\Gamma},\rho}\right)^{-1}\circ\overline{F}(h_{\Gamma}^{02})\tau_{f_{3}}^{A}\circ\left(\phi_{h_{\Gamma}^{02},f_{3}}^{A}\right)^{-1}\circ\phi_{f,h_{\Gamma^{\prime}}^{02}}^{A}.

Proving this is an extremely tedious but routine verification that follows from the pseudofunctoriality of
\overline{F}
and the induced relations provided by the pasting diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{F}(\,_{\Gamma_{3}^{\prime}}{X})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{F}(\,_{\Gamma_{2}^{\prime}}{X})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{F}(\,_{\Gamma_{1}^{\prime}}{X})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{F}(\,_{\Gamma}{X})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{F}(\,_{\Gamma_{3}}{X})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{F}(\,_{\Gamma_{2}}{X})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{F}(\,_{\Gamma_{1}}{X})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{F}(\,_{\Gamma}{X})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{F}(h_{\Gamma^{\prime}}^{02})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{F}(\overline{\rho}^{\prime})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{F}(\of_{3})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{F}(h_{\Gamma^{\prime}}^{12})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{F}(\of_{2})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{F}(\of_{1})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{F}(h_{\Gamma^{\prime}}^{01})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{F}(\of)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{F}(h_{\Gamma}^{02})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{F}(\overline{\rho})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{F}(h_{\Gamma_{1}}^{12})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{F}(h_{\Gamma}^{01})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\phi_{h_{\Gamma_{1}^{\prime}\circ h_{\Gamma^{\prime},\rho}}}\ast\phi_{h_{\Gamma^{\prime}},h_{\Gamma_{1}^{\prime}}})^{-1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi_{h_{\Gamma_{1}}\circ h_{\Gamma},\rho}\ast\phi_{h_{\Gamma},h_{\Gamma_{1}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi_{f_{2},\rho^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\phi_{\rho,f_{3}})^{-1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi_{f_{1},h_{\Gamma^{\prime}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\phi_{h_{\Gamma_{1}},f_{2}})^{-1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi_{f,h_{\Gamma^{\prime}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\phi_{h_{\Gamma},f_{1}})^{-1}}

and the fact that it is equal to the invertible
2
-cell:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{F}(\,_{\Gamma_{3}^{\prime}}{X})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{F}(\XGammap)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{F}(\,_{\Gamma_{3}}{X})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{F}(\XGamma)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{F}(h_{\Gamma^{\prime}}^{02})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{F}(\of_{3})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{F}(\of)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{F}(h_{\Gamma}^{02})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi_{h_{\Gamma}^{02},f_{3}}^{-1}\circ\phi_{f,h_{\Gamma^{\prime}}^{02}}}

Doing this shows that the two morphisms are indeed equivalent and establishes that

\tau_{f}^{\varphi^{\sharp}(\psi^{\sharp}A)}\circ\overline{F}(\overline{f})\,_{\Gamma^{\prime}}{\alpha_{A}}=\,_{\Gamma}{\alpha_{A}}\circ\tau_{f}^{(\psi\circ\varphi)^{\sharp}A},

which in turn completes the verification that
\alpha_{A}:(\psi\circ\varphi)^{\sharp}A\to\varphi^{\sharp}(\psi^{\sharp}A)
is a morphism in
\overline{F}_{G_{0}}(X)
.

We finish by verifying the naturality of
\alpha
, i.e., that for any morphism

\Sigma=\{\,_{\Gamma}{\sigma}\;|\;\Gamma\in\Sf(G_{2})_{0}\}\in\overline{F}_{G_{2}}(X)(A,B)

the diagram

\textstyle{(\psi\circ\varphi)^{\sharp}A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\alpha_{A}}
\scriptstyle{(\psi\circ\varphi)^{\sharp}\Sigma}
\textstyle{\varphi^{\sharp}(\psi^{\sharp}A)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varphi^{\sharp}(\psi^{\sharp}\Sigma)}
\textstyle{(\psi\circ\varphi)^{\sharp}B\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\alpha_{B}}
\textstyle{\varphi^{\sharp}(\psi^{\sharp}B)}

commutes. Now observe that since
\Sigma
is an
\Sf(G_{2})
-morphism, for any object
\Gamma
of
\Sf(G_{0})
the diagram

\textstyle{\overline{F}(\overline{\rho})\,_{\Gamma_{3}}{A}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\overline{F}(\overline{\rho})\,_{\Gamma_{3}}{\sigma}}
\scriptstyle{\tau_{\rho}^{A}}
\textstyle{\overline{F}(\overline{\rho})\,_{\Gamma_{3}}{B}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\tau_{\rho}^{B}}
\textstyle{\,{}_{\Gamma_{2}}{A}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sigma_{\Gamma_{2}}}
\textstyle{\,{}_{\Gamma_{2}}{B}}

commutes. Using this we verify that

\,{}_{\Gamma}{\alpha_{B}}\circ\,_{\Gamma}{(\psi\circ\varphi)^{\sharp}\Sigma}

\displaystyle=\left(\overline{F}(h_{\Gamma}^{01})\circ\overline{F}(h_{\Gamma_{1}}^{12})\right)\tau_{\rho}^{B}\circ\left(\phi_{h_{\Gamma},h_{\Gamma_{1}}}^{\overline{F}(\overline{\rho})B}\right)^{-1}\circ\left(\phi_{h_{\Gamma_{1}}\circ h_{\Gamma},\rho}^{B}\right)^{-1}\circ\overline{F}(h_{\Gamma}^{02})\,_{\Gamma_{3}}{\sigma}

\displaystyle=\left(\overline{F}(h_{\Gamma}^{01})\circ\overline{F}(h_{\Gamma_{1}}^{12})\right)\tau_{\rho}^{B}\circ\left(\phi_{h_{\Gamma},h_{\Gamma_{1}}}^{\overline{F}(\overline{\rho})B}\right)^{-1}\circ\left(\phi_{h_{\Gamma_{1}}\circ h_{\Gamma},\rho}^{B}\right)^{-1}\circ\overline{F}(\overline{\rho}\circ h_{\Gamma_{1}}^{12}\circ h_{\Gamma}^{01})\,_{\Gamma_{3}}{\sigma}

\displaystyle=\left(\overline{F}(h_{\Gamma}^{01})\circ\overline{F}(h_{\Gamma_{1}}^{12})\right)\tau_{\rho}^{B}\circ\left(\phi_{h_{\Gamma},h_{\Gamma_{1}}}^{\overline{F}(\overline{\rho})B}\right)^{-1}\circ\left(\overline{F}(h_{\Gamma_{1}}^{12}\circ h_{\Gamma}^{01})\circ\overline{F}(\overline{\rho})\right)\,_{\Gamma_{3}}{\sigma}

\displaystyle\circ\left(\phi_{h_{\Gamma_{1}}\circ h_{\Gamma},\rho}^{A}\right)^{-1}

\displaystyle=\left(\overline{F}(h_{\Gamma}^{01})\circ\overline{F}(h_{\Gamma_{1}}^{12})\right)\tau_{\rho}^{B}\circ\left(\overline{F}(h_{\Gamma}^{01})\circ\overline{F}(h_{\Gamma_{1}}^{12})\circ\overline{F}(\overline{\rho})\right)\,_{\Gamma_{3}}{\sigma}\circ\left(\phi_{h_{\Gamma},h_{\Gamma_{1}}}^{\overline{F}(\overline{\rho})A}\right)^{-1}

\displaystyle\circ\left(\phi_{h_{\Gamma_{1}}\circ h_{\Gamma},\rho}^{A}\right)^{-1}

\displaystyle=\left(\overline{F}(h_{\Gamma}^{01})\circ\overline{F}(h_{\Gamma_{1}}^{12})\right)\left(\tau_{\rho}^{B}\circ\overline{F}(\overline{\rho})\,_{\Gamma_{3}}{\sigma}\right)\circ\left(\phi_{h_{\Gamma},h_{\Gamma_{1}}}^{\overline{F}(\overline{\rho})A}\right)^{-1}\circ\left(\phi_{h_{\Gamma_{1}}\circ h_{\Gamma},\rho}^{A}\right)^{-1}

\displaystyle=\left(\overline{F}(h_{\Gamma}^{01})\circ\overline{F}(h_{\Gamma_{1}}^{12})\right)\left(\,{}_{\Gamma_{2}}{\sigma}\circ\tau_{\rho}^{A}\right)\circ\left(\phi_{h_{\Gamma},h_{\Gamma_{1}}}^{\overline{F}(\overline{\rho})A}\right)^{-1}\circ\left(\phi_{h_{\Gamma_{1}}\circ h_{\Gamma},\rho}^{A}\right)^{-1}

\displaystyle=\left(\overline{F}(h_{\Gamma}^{01})\circ\overline{F}(h_{\Gamma_{1}}^{12})\right)\,_{\Gamma_{2}}{\sigma}\circ\left(\overline{F}(h_{\Gamma}^{01})\circ\overline{F}(h_{\Gamma_{1}}^{12})\right)\tau_{\rho}^{A}\circ\left(\phi_{h_{\Gamma},h_{\Gamma_{1}}}^{\overline{F}(\overline{\rho})A}\right)^{-1}\circ\left(\phi_{h_{\Gamma_{1}}\circ h_{\Gamma},\rho}^{A}\right)^{-1}

\displaystyle=\,_{\Gamma}{\varphi^{\sharp}(\psi^{\sharp}\Sigma)}\circ\,_{\Gamma}{\alpha_{A}},

which establishes the naturality of
\alpha
.
∎

We now discuss the de-equivariantification functor, which is a Change of Groups analog of the forgetful functor of Proposition
6.1.17
, and then give an explicit comparison between the functor
1_{G}^{\sharp}:F_{G}(X)\to F_{\Spec K}(X)
(respectively
1_{G}^{\sharp}:F_{G}(X)\to F_{\{\ast\}}(X)
in the topological case) and
\Forget:F_{G}(X)\to\overline{F}(X)
. This comparison is a useful consequence of the change of groups formalism; it gives rise to a functor
F_{G}(X)\to F(X)
for any group
G
and any space
X
. Since we always have a morphism of algebraic groups
1_{G}:\Spec K\to G
for any algebraic group
G
, from Theorem
7.1.13
we get a functor
1_{G}^{\sharp}:F_{G}(X)\to F_{\Spec K}(X)
that restricts along this inclusion of the identity of
G
. Recall that there is an equivalence of categories
F_{\Spec K}(X)\simeq\overline{F}(X)
(cf. Proposition
2.3.13
); let
\overline{E}:F_{\Spec K}(X)\to\overline{F}(X)
be one such functor witnessing the equivalence. We then obtain a functor

F_{G}(X)\xrightarrow{1_{G}^{\sharp}}F_{\Spec K}(X)\xrightarrow{\overline{E}}\overline{F}(X)

which suitably “de-equivariantifies” the category
F_{G}(X)
; note that in the topological case this is the functor:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{G}(X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{\{\ast\}}(X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{F}(X)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1_{G}^{\sharp}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{E}}

We call the corresponding functor
\overline{E}\circ 1_{G}^{\sharp}:F_{G}(X)\to\overline{F}(X)
or its topological companion a de-equivariantification functor. In fact, this can be seen as an explanation using the Change of Groups formalism exactly what the forgetful functor
\Forget:F_{G}(X)\to F(X)
is doing in a group-theoretic sense. While the proof we present below is functionally very similar to that of Proposition
7.1.18
above, we present it in detail below to illustrate how the forgetful functor may be seen as a Change of Groups in disguise.

Proposition 7.1.19
.

Let
G
be a smooth algebraic group and let
X
be a left
G
-variety with unit map
1_{G}:\Spec K\to G
. Then for any pre-equivariant pseudofunctor
F
on
X
there is an invertible
2
-cell:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{G}(X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{\Spec K}(X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{F}(X)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1_{G}^{\sharp}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Forget}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{E}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha}

Similarly, if
G
is a topological group with unit map
1_{G}:\{\ast\}\to G
and
X
is a left
G
-space then for any pre-equivariant pseudofunctor on
X
there is an invertible
2
-cell:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{G}(X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{\{\ast\}}(X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{F}(X)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1_{G}^{\sharp}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Forget}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{E}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha}

Proof.

We begin by observing that there is a
G
-equivariant isomorphism of varieites in
\Sf(G)
of the form

G\times^{\Spec K}\Spec K\cong G

(this can be seen in set-theoretic terms via the mappings
[g,1]_{1}\mapsto g,g\mapsto[g,1]_{1}
), and this isomorphism induces the further isomorphism

\,{}_{G\times^{\Spec K}\Spec K}{X}\xrightarrow[\rho]{\cong}\,_{G}{X}.

Now consider the isomorphism

h_{\Spec K}:\,_{\Spec K}{X}\xrightarrow{\cong}\,_{G\times^{\Spec K}\Spec K}{X}

and write the isomorphism from
X
to
\,{}_{\Spec K}{X}
as

X\xrightarrow[\varphi]{\cong}\,_{\Spec K}{X}.

Finally, let
\psi:X\to\,_{G}{X}
be the standard (non-
G
-equivariant) isomorphism of varieties. Let us now observe that on one hand we have that for any object
A\in F_{G}(X)_{0}

\displaystyle\overline{E}(1_{G}^{\sharp}(A))

\displaystyle=\overline{E}\left(\left\{\overline{F}(h_{\Gamma})\left(\,{}_{G\times^{\Spec K}\Gamma}{A}\right)\;:\;\Gamma\in\Sf(G)_{0}\right\}\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\overline{F}(\varphi)\circ\overline{F}(h_{\Spec K})\right)\left(\,{}_{G\times^{\Spec K}\Spec K}{A}\right)

and similarly for morphisms. On the other hand we have that

\Forget(A)=\overline{F}(\psi)(\,_{G}{A}).

Now since the diagram

\textstyle{\,{}_{G\times^{\Spec K}\Spec K}{X}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\overline{\rho}}
\textstyle{\,{}_{\Spec K}{X}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{h_{\Spec K}}
\textstyle{\,{}_{G}{X}}
\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\psi}
\scriptstyle{\varphi}

of varieties commutes by the uniqueness of isomorphisms between quotient schemes, we have the following pasting diagram (where we implicitly use that on quotient varieties
F=\overline{F}
)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{F}(\,_{\Spec K}{X})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{F}\left(\,{}_{G\times^{\Spec K}\Spec K}{X}\right)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{F}(X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{F}\left(\,{}_{G}{X}\right)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{F}(\varphi)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{F}(h_{\Spec K}\circ\varphi)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{F}(h_{\Spec K})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{F}(\psi)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{F}(\overline{\rho})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi_{h\circ\varphi,\rho}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi_{\varphi,h}}

in
\fCat
. Notice that because
\rho\in\Sf(G)_{1}
, we have an isomorphism

\tau_{\rho}^{A}:\overline{F}(\overline{\rho})\,_{G}{A}\xrightarrow{\cong}\,_{(G\times\Spec K)_{\Spec K}}{A}

in
T_{A}
. Applying
\overline{F}(\varphi)\circ\overline{F}(h_{\Spec K})
to this isomorphism gives us an isomorphism

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left(\overline{F}(\varphi)\circ\overline{F}(h_{\Spec K})\right)\left(\,{}_{G\times^{\Spec K}\Spec K}{A}\right)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left(F(\varphi)\circ F(h_{\Spec K})\circ F(\overline{\rho})\right)\left(\,{}_{G}{A}\right)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\overline{E}\circ 1_{G}^{\sharp})(A)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{F}(\varphi)(F(h_{\Spec K})\tau_{\rho}^{A})}

To get our isomorphism to have the desired domain of

\Forget(A)=\overline{F}(\psi)\left(\,{}_{G}{A}\right)=\overline{F}(\overline{\rho}\circ h_{\Spec K}\circ\varphi)\left(\,{}_{G}{A}\right),

we pre-compose by the inverses of the two natural isomorphisms to produce the isomorphism

\alpha_{A}:=\left(\overline{F}(\varphi)\circ\overline{F}(h_{\Spec K})\right)\tau_{\rho}^{A}\circ\left(\phi_{\varphi,h}^{F(\overline{\rho})\,_{G}{A}}\right)^{-1}\circ\left(\phi_{h\circ\varphi,\rho}^{\,{}_{G}{A}}\right)^{-1}.

Then
\alpha_{A}:\Forget(A)\xrightarrow{\cong}(\overline{F}\circ 1_{G}^{\sharp})(A)
, as desired.

We end by showing that
\alpha
is natural in
F_{G}(X)
, i.e.,that for a morphism

\Sigma=\{\,_{\Gamma}{\sigma}\;|\;\Gamma\in\Sf(G)_{0}\}\in F_{G}(X)(A,B)

that the diagram

\textstyle{\Forget(A)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\alpha_{A}}
\scriptstyle{\Forget(\Sigma)}
\textstyle{(\overline{F}\circ 1_{G}^{\sharp})(A)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\overline{F}\circ 1_{G}^{\sharp})(\Sigma)}
\textstyle{\Forget(B)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\alpha_{B}}
\textstyle{(\overline{F}\circ 1_{G}^{\sharp})(B)}

commutes. First recall that since
\Sigma
is an
F_{G}(X)
-morphisms, the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(\overline{\rho})(\,_{G}{A})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(\overline{\rho})\,_{G}{B}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\,{}_{G\times^{\Spec K}\Spec K}{A}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\,{}_{G\times^{\Spec K}\Spec K}{B}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau_{\rho}^{A}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(\overline{\rho})\,_{G}{\sigma}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau_{\rho}^{B}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\,{}_{G\times^{\Spec K}\Spec K}{\sigma}}

commutes in
\overline{F}(\,_{G\times^{\Spec K}\Spec K}{X}).
We then conclude the proof by calculating

\displaystyle(\overline{E}\circ 1_{G}^{\sharp})(\Sigma)\circ\alpha_{A}

\displaystyle=\left(\overline{F}(\varphi)\circ\overline{F}(h_{\Spec K})\right)\left(\,{}_{G\times^{\Spec K}\Spec K}{\sigma}\right)\circ\left(\overline{F}(\varphi)\circ\overline{F}(h_{\Spec K})\right)\tau_{\rho}^{A}

\displaystyle\circ\left(\phi_{\varphi,h}^{F(\overline{\rho})\,_{G}{A}}\right)^{-1}\circ\left(\phi_{h\circ\varphi,\rho}^{\,{}_{G}{A}}\right)^{-1}

\displaystyle=\left(\overline{F}(\varphi)\circ\overline{F}(h_{\Spec K})\right)\left(\,{}_{G\times^{\Spec K}\Spec K}{\sigma}\circ\tau_{\rho}^{A}\right)\circ\left(\phi_{\varphi,h}^{F(\overline{\rho})\,_{G}{A}}\right)^{-1}\circ\left(\phi_{h\circ\varphi,\rho}^{\,{}_{G}{A}}\right)^{-1}

\displaystyle=\left(\overline{F}(\varphi)\circ\overline{F}(h_{\Spec K})\right)\left(\tau_{\rho}^{B}\circ F(\overline{\rho})\,_{G}{\sigma}\right)\circ\left(\phi_{\varphi,h}^{F(\overline{\rho})\,_{G}{A}}\right)^{-1}\circ\left(\phi_{h\circ\varphi,\rho}^{\,{}_{G}{A}}\right)^{-1}

\displaystyle=\left(\overline{F}(\varphi)\circ\overline{F}(h_{\Spec K})\right)(\tau_{\rho}^{B})\circ\left(\phi_{\varphi,h}^{F(\overline{\rho})\,_{G}{B}}\right)^{-1}\circ\left(\phi_{h\circ\varphi,\rho}^{\,{}_{G}{B}}\right)^{-1}\circ\overline{F}(\psi)\,_{G}{\sigma}

\displaystyle=\alpha_{B}\circ\Forget(\Sigma).

∎

7.2.
A High-Level Perspective On Change of Groups Functors

In this section we give a high-level perspective on the functors we have just developed and studied. Because of the high degree of technical information involved in the construction Change of Group functors, it can be easy to lose ourselves in the weeds; as such, we should have a
2
-category and a pseudofunctor or two running around to help us record exactly what those details are describing and recording. With this in mind, we start this section with a goal of introducing a
2
-category
({G},{H})\mathfrak{PreEq}(\mathnormal{X})
of
(G,H)
-pre-equivariant pseudofunctors on a variety
X
for fixed algebraic groups
G,H
with a morphism
\varphi:G\to H
(respectively of
(G,H)
-pre-equivariant pseudofunctors on an
H
-space
X
for fixed topological groups
G,H
) before describing a more general
2
-category
({-},{H})\mathfrak{PreEq}(\mathnormal{X})
which leaves only
H
fixed. Consequently, we need to have a notion of morphism between
(G,H)
-pre-equivariant pseudofunctors as well as transformations between such morphisms.

Definition 7.2.1
.

Let
G
,
H
,
\varphi:G\to H
, and
X
be as in either of the cases below:

(1)

G
and
H
are smooth algebraic groups over a field
K
,
X
is a left
H
-variety, and
\varphi
is an algebraic group morphism
\varphi:G\to H
;

(2)

G
and
H
are topological groups,
X
is a left
H
-space, and
\varphi
is a topological group morphism
\varphi:G\to H
.

If
\underline{{F}}_{1}`=\left((F_{1},\overline{F}_{1}),(F_{1}^{\prime},\overline{F}_{1}^{\prime}),e_{1}\right)
and
\underline{{F}}_{2}=\left((F_{2},\overline{F}_{2}),(F_{2}^{\prime},\overline{F}_{2}^{\prime}),e_{2}\right)
are
(G,H)
-pre-equivariant pseudofunctors on
X
then a morphism
\alpha:\underline{{F}}_{1}\to\underline{{F}}_{2}
of
(G,H)
-pre-equivariant pseudofunctors is a quadruple
\alpha=(\alpha,\overline{\alpha},\alpha^{\prime},\overline{\alpha}^{\prime})
where:

•

\alpha:F_{1}\Rightarrow F_{2},\overline{\alpha}:\overline{F}_{1}\Rightarrow\overline{F}_{2},\alpha^{\prime}:F_{1}^{\prime}\Rightarrow F_{2}^{\prime}
, and
\overline{\alpha}^{\prime}:\overline{F}_{1}^{\prime}\Rightarrow\overline{F}_{2}^{\prime}
are pseudonatural transformations.

•

The whiskering identities
\alpha=\overline{\alpha}\ast\quo_{H}^{\op}
and
\alpha^{\prime}=\overline{\alpha}^{\prime}\ast\quo_{G}^{\op}
hold.

•

The diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{F}_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{F}_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{F}^{\prime}_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{F}^{\prime}_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\alpha}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\alpha}^{\prime}}

of pseudofunctors and pseudonatural transformations commutes.

Immediate from the definition and Theorem
4.1.1
is the following lemma.

Lemma 7.2.2
.

A morphism
\alpha
of
(G,H)
-pre-equivariant pseudofunctors gives rise to functors
(F_{1})_{G}(X)\to(F_{2})_{G}(X)
,
\PC(\overline{F}_{1})\to\PC(\overline{F}_{2})
,
(F_{1}^{\prime})_{H}(X)\to(F_{2}^{\prime})_{H}(X)
, and
\PC(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{1})\to\PC(\overline{F}_{2}^{\prime})
for which the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\PC(\overline{F}_{1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\PC(\overline{F}_{2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\PC(\overline{F}_{1}^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\PC(\overline{F}^{\prime}_{2})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\PC(\overline{\alpha})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\PC(e_{1})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\PC(e_{2})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\PC(\overline{\alpha}^{\prime})}

of categories and functors commutes.

Definition 7.2.3
.

Let
G
,
H
,
X
, and
\varphi
all be given as in Cases 1 or 2 of Definition
7.2.1
. Let
\underline{{F}}_{1}`=\left((F_{1},\overline{F}_{1}),(F_{1}^{\prime},\overline{F}_{1}^{\prime}),e_{1}\right)
and
\underline{{F}}_{2}=\left((F_{2},\overline{F}_{2}),(F_{2}^{\prime},\overline{F}_{2}^{\prime}),e_{2}\right)
be
(G,H)
-pre-equivariant pseudofunctors on
X
with morphisms
\alpha,\beta:\underline{{F}}_{1}\to\underline{{F}}_{2}
of
(G,H)
-pre-equivariant pseudofunctors. A transformation
\rho:\alpha\Rightarrow\beta
is a quadruple
\rho=(\rho,\overline{\rho},\rho^{\prime},\overline{\rho})
where:

•

\rho:\alpha\Rrightarrow\beta,\overline{\rho}:\overline{\alpha}\Rrightarrow\overline{\beta},\rho^{\prime}:\alpha^{\prime}\Rrightarrow\beta^{\prime}
, and
\overline{\rho}^{\prime}:\overline{\alpha}^{\prime}\Rrightarrow\overline{\beta}^{\prime}
are modifications.

•

The whiskering identity
e_{2}\ast\overline{\rho}=\overline{\rho}^{\prime}\ast e_{1}
holds.

•

The whiskering identities
\rho=\overline{\rho}\ast\iota_{\quo_{G}^{\op}}
and
\rho^{\prime}=\overline{\rho}^{\prime}\ast\iota_{\quo_{G}^{\op}}
both hold.

We, similar to Lemma
7.2.2
above, derive the following lemma immediately from applying Lemma
4.1.11
to the definition above.

Lemma 7.2.4
.

A transformation
\rho:\alpha\Rightarrow\beta
between
(G,H)
-pre-equivariant pseudofunctor morphisms
\alpha,\beta:\underline{{F}}_{1}\to\underline{{F}}_{2}
give rise to natural transformations

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(F_{1})_{G}(X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(F_{2})_{G}(X)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\beta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(F_{1}^{\prime})_{H}(X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(F_{2}^{\prime})_{H}(X)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\beta^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho^{\prime}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\PC(\overline{F}_{1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\PC(\overline{F}_{2})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\PC(\overline{\alpha})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\PC(\overline{\beta})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\PC(\overline{\rho})}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\PC(\overline{F}_{1}^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\PC(\overline{F}_{2})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\PC(\overline{\alpha}^{\prime})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\PC(\overline{\beta}^{\prime})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\PC(\overline{\rho}^{\prime})}

for which the identity
\PC(\overline{\rho}^{\prime})\ast\PC(e_{1})=\PC(e_{2})\ast\PC(\overline{\rho})
holds.

A straightforward consequence of these constructions is that it allows us to organize all three pieces of information into a
2
-category
({G},{H})\mathfrak{PreEq}(\mathnormal{X})
of
(G,H)
-pre-equivariant pseudofunctors on
X
.

Proposition 7.2.5
.

Let
K
be a field,
G
and
H
be smooth algebraic groups over
K
with
X
a left
H
-variety and
\varphi:G\to H
a morphism of smooth algebraic groups. Then there is a
2
-category
({G},{H})\mathfrak{PreEq}(\mathnormal{X})
of
(G,H)
-pre-equivariant pseudofunctors on
X
where:

•

The
0
-cells are
(G,H)
-pre-equivariant pseudofunctors;

•

The
1
-cells are morphisms of
(G,H)
-pre-equivariant pseudofunctors;

•

The
2
-cells are transformations of morphisms of
(G,H)
-pre-equivariant pseudofunctors;

•

Composition of
1
-cells is induced by the vertical composition of pseudonatural transformations;

•

Composition of
2
-cells is induced by the composition of
2
-cells in the
2
-categories
\Bicat(-,\fCat)
.

Proof.

Fix two
(G,H)
-pre-equivariant pseudofunctors
\underline{{F}}_{1}
and
\underline{{F}}_{2}
. Define the hom-category
({G},{H})\mathfrak{PreEq}(\mathnormal{X})(\underline{{F}}_{1},\underline{{F}}_{2})
as follows:

•

Objects: morphisms
\alpha:\underline{{F}}_{1}\to\underline{{F}}_{2}
.

•

Morphisms: transformations
\rho:\alpha\to\beta
.

•

Composition: Given morphisms
\alpha,\beta,\gamma:\underline{{F}}_{1}\to\underline{{F}}_{2}
with transformations
\varphi:\alpha\to\beta
and
\psi:\beta\to\gamma
, we define

\psi\circ\varphi:=\left(\psi\circ\varphi,\overline{\psi}\circ\overline{\varphi},\psi^{\prime}\circ\varphi^{\prime},\overline{\psi}^{\prime}\circ\overline{\varphi}^{\prime}\right).

•

Identities: Given a morphism
\alpha:\underline{{F}}_{1}\to\underline{{F}}_{2}
, the identity transformation is
\iota_{\alpha}=(\iota_{\alpha},\iota_{\overline{\alpha}},\iota_{\alpha^{\prime}},\iota_{\overline{\alpha}^{\prime}})
.

That this is a category is trivial to verify, as the fact that composition in the assignment
({G},{H})\mathfrak{PreEq}(\mathnormal{X})(\underline{{F}}_{1},\underline{{F}}_{2})
is strictly associative in the geometric case follows from the fact that each of the three bicategories
\Bicat(\SfResl_{G}(X)^{\op},\fCat)
,
\Bicat(\SfResl_{H}(X)^{\op},\fCat)
, and
\Bicat(\Var_{/K}^{\op},\fCat)
are actually
2
-categories; in the topological case that composition is strictly associative follows from the fact that the three bicategories
\Bicat(\mathbf{FRes}_{G}(X)^{\op},\fCat)
,
\Bicat(\mathbf{FRes}_{H}(X)^{\op},\fCat)
, and
\Bicat(\Top^{\op},\fCat)
are all
2
-categories.

Alternatively, if we have
(G,H)
-pre-equivariant pseudofunctors
\underline{{F}}_{1},\underline{{F}}_{2}
, and
\underline{{F}}_{3}
on
X
then the horizontal composition functors

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces({G},{H})\mathfrak{PreEq}(\mathnormal{X})(\underline{{F}}_{2},\underline{{F}}_{3})\times({G},{H})\mathfrak{PreEq}(\mathnormal{X})(\underline{{F}}_{1},\underline{{F}}_{2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces({G},{H})\mathfrak{PreEq}(\mathnormal{X})(\underline{{F}}_{1},\underline{{F}}_{3})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ast}

are defined by taking, for each pair of morphisms
\alpha:\underline{{F}}_{1}\to\underline{{F}}_{2}
and
\beta:\underline{{F}}_{2}\to\underline{{F}}_{3}
,

\ast(\beta,\alpha)=\left(\beta\ast\alpha,\overline{\beta}\ast\overline{\alpha},\beta^{\prime}\ast\alpha^{\prime},\overline{\beta}^{\prime}\ast\overline{\alpha}^{\prime}\right)

as the object assignment (where the horizontal composition is defined as at the beginning of Section
4.1
) and

\ast(\psi,\varphi)=\left(\psi\ast\varphi,\overline{\psi}\ast\overline{\varphi},\psi^{\prime}\ast\varphi^{\prime},\overline{\psi}^{\prime}\ast\overline{\varphi}^{\prime}\right)

for transformations
\varphi:\alpha\Rightarrow\beta:\underline{{F}}_{1}\to\underline{{F}}_{2}
and
\psi:\gamma\Rightarrow\delta:\underline{{F}}_{2}\to\underline{{F}}_{3}
; note that the horizontal composition of modifications is also defined at the beginning of Section
4.1
. Then because each bicategory
\Bicat(\SfResl_{G}(X)^{\op},\fCat)
,

\Bicat(\SfResl_{H}(X)^{\op},\fCat)
, and
\Bicat(\Var_{/K}^{\op},\fCat)
is a
2
-category (and similarly for the topological case), it follows that
\ast
is a functor. In particular, this allows us to deduce that
({G},{H})\mathfrak{PreEq}(\mathnormal{X})
is a category enriched in
\Cat
and hence is a
2
-category.
∎

Remark 7.2.6
.

Following Remark
1.3.1
, we will write
(G,H)\PreEq(X)
for the
1
-category of
(G,H)
-pre-equivariant pseudofunctors and their morphisms.

A straightforward translation of the proof above allows us to construct two further
2
-categories useful in the study of Change of Groups functors. The first of these
2
-categories is in essence the
2
-category of
(G,H)
-pre-equivariant pseudofunctors on
X
where we allow
G
to vary through all the algebraic groups (respectively topological groups) and structure maps
\varphi:G\to H
appearing in the category
\mathbf{SAlgGrp}_{/K}\downarrow H
(respectively also the category
\mathbf{TopGrp}\downarrow H
; cf. Remark
1.3.1
for the definition of the categories
\mathbf{SAlgGrp}_{/K}
and
\mathbf{TopGrp}\downarrow H
) while the second is the
2
-category generated by all
(G,G^{\prime})
-pre-equivariant pseudofunctors as we vary through morphisms
\varphi:G\to G^{\prime}
in
\mathbf{SAlgGrp}\downarrow H
(respectively in
\mathbf{TopGrp}\downarrow H
).

Definition 7.2.7
.

Let
G,H
, and
X
be given as in either cases:

•

G
is an arbitrary smooth algebraic group over
K
and for which there is a morphism
\varphi:G\to H
of smooth algebraic groups and
X
is a left
H
-variety;

•

G
is an arbitrary topological group for which there is a morphism
\varphi:G\to H
of topological groups and
X
is a left
H
-space.

The
2
-category
({-},{H})\mathfrak{PreEq}(\mathnormal{X})
is defined as follows:

•

The
0
-cells of
({-},{H})\mathfrak{PreEq}(\mathnormal{X})
are
(G,H)
-pre-equivariant pseudofunctors on
X
in either case:

More succinctly,

({-},{H})\mathfrak{PreEq}(\mathnormal{X})_{0}=\bigcup_{G\in(\mathbf{SAlgGrp}_{/K}\downarrow H)_{0}}({G},{H})\mathfrak{PreEq}(\mathnormal{X}).

•

The
1
-cells of
({-},{H})\mathfrak{PreEq}(\mathnormal{X})
are generated by

({-},{H})\mathfrak{PreEq}(\mathnormal{X})_{1}=\left\langle\bigcup_{G\in(\mathbf{SAlgGrp}_{/K}\downarrow H)_{0}}({G},{H})\mathfrak{PreEq}(\mathnormal{X})_{1}\right\rangle.

•

The
2
-cells are generated by

({-},{H})\mathfrak{PreEq}(\mathnormal{X})_{2}=\left\langle\bigcup_{G\in(\mathbf{SAlgGrp}_{/K}\downarrow H)_{0}}({G},{H})\mathfrak{PreEq}(\mathnormal{X})_{2}\right\rangle.

Remark 7.2.8
.

For any groups
G,G^{\prime}
over
H
with a morphism
\varphi:G\to G^{\prime}
, we can equip the
H
-object
X
with the pullback action against the structure maps
\nu_{G}:G\to H
and
\nu_{G^{\prime}}:G^{\prime}\to H
in order to produce the category
({G},{G^{\prime}})\mathfrak{PreEq}(\mathnormal{X})
; note that the subtlety here lies in equipping
X
with
G^{\prime}
and
G
actions which are suitably compatible with each other through the pullback action of
G
on
X
as induced through the map
\varphi:G\to G^{\prime}
(which, in this case, works out exactly because
\varphi
is a morphism in the category of groups over
H
, giving that the equation
\nu_{G^{\prime}}\circ\varphi=\nu_{G}
holds).

Our main goal for the moment is to show that the Change of Groups functors are pseudofunctorial by using
({-},{H})\mathfrak{PreEq}(\mathnormal{X})
as a tool to mediate and describe exactly what it is that Change of Groups functors are doing. We first record that the
2
-category
({-},{H})\mathfrak{PreEq}(\mathnormal{X})
arises as the elements of the pseudofunctor
(\mathbf{SAlgGrp}_{/K}\downarrow H)^{\op}\to\fCat
which sends an algebraic group to its
1
-category of
(G,H)
-pre-equivariant pseudofunctors.

Lemma 7.2.9
.

Let
H
and
X
be given as in either of the two cases below:

(1)

H
is a smooth algebraic group and
X
is a left
H
-variety.

(2)

H
is a topological group and
X
is a left
H
-space.

There are pseudofunctors

\Ebb:(\mathbf{SAlgGrp}_{/K}\downarrow H)^{\op}\to\fCat

and

\Fbb:(\mathbf{TopGrp}_{/K}\downarrow H)^{\op}\to\fCat

defined by

\Ebb(G):=(G,H)\PreEq(X)

and

\Fbb(G):=(G,H)\PreEq(X)

on objects. For morphisms
\varphi:G\to G^{\prime}
, the fibre functors

\Ebb(\varphi):=\varphi^{\natural}:(G^{\prime},H)\PreEq(X)\to(G,H)\PreEq(X)

and

\Fbb(\varphi):=\varphi^{\natural}:(G^{\prime},H)\PreEq(X)\to(G,H)\PreEq(X)

are defined by sending a
(G^{\prime},H)
-pre-equivariant pseudofunctor
\left((F,\overline{F}),(F^{\prime},\overline{F}^{\prime}),e\right)
to the
(G,H)
-pre-equivariant pseudofunctor

\left(\left(F\circ\left(G^{\prime}\times^{G}(-)\right)^{\op},\overline{F}\right),(F^{\prime},\overline{F}^{\prime}),e\right)

and similarly on morphisms.

Proof.

As usual, we present the geometric argument as the topological argument is carried out mutatis mutandis to the geometric one. We first need to type-check
\varphi^{\natural}
. However, note that since
G^{\prime}\times^{G}(-):\SfResl_{G}(X)\to\SfResl_{G^{\prime}}(X)
is a functor, by construction
(F\circ(G\times^{G}(-))^{\op},\overline{F})
is a
G
-pre-equivariant pseudofunctor on
X
; that it fits into the
(G,H)
-pre-equivariant pseudofunctor claimed follows by construction as well. Finally, that this is pseudofunctorial in
\mathbf{SAlgGrp}_{/K}\downarrow H
follows from the natural isomorphisms

G^{\prime\prime}\times^{G^{\prime}}\left(G^{\prime}\times^{G}(-)\right)\cong G^{\prime\prime}\times^{G}(-)

of
G^{\prime\prime}
-varieties for any composable morphisms
G\xrightarrow{\varphi}G^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\psi}G^{\prime\prime}
in
\mathbf{SAlgGrp}\downarrow H
.
∎

In what follows we will examine to what degree the Change of Groups functors are associative. The main task in front of us is to verify that if we have algebraic group (respectively topological group) morphisms

G_{0}\xrightarrow{\varphi_{01}}G_{1}\xrightarrow{\varphi_{12}}G_{2}\xrightarrow{\varphi_{23}}G_{3}

then for any left
G_{3}
-object
X
and any
G_{3}
-pre-equivariant pseudofunctor

on
X
, the pasting diagram

(7.2.1)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{G_{2}}(X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{G_{3}}(X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{G_{1}}(X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{G_{0}}(X)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{12}^{\sharp}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{23}^{\sharp}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\varphi_{23}\circ\varphi_{12}\circ\varphi_{01})^{\sharp}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\varphi_{23}\circ\varphi_{12})^{\sharp}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{01}^{\sharp}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha_{0,123}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha_{123}}

is equal to the pasting diagram:

(7.2.2)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{G_{2}}(X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{G_{3}}(X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{G_{1}}(X)}

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{12}^{\sharp}}

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{01}^{\sharp}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha_{012}}

However, because of the nature of each of these functors and constructions involving successive induction space constructions and cancelations (such as the examples

\displaystyle G_{3}\times^{G_{2}}\left(G_{2}\times^{G_{1}}\left(G_{1}\times^{G_{0}}\Gamma\right)\right)

\displaystyle\qquad G_{3}\times^{G_{0}}\Gamma

\displaystyle G_{2}\times^{G_{1}}\left(G_{1}\times^{G_{0}}\Gamma\right)

\displaystyle\qquad G_{3}\times^{G_{1}}\left(G_{1}\times^{G_{0}}\Gamma\right)

\displaystyle G_{3}\times^{G_{2}}\left(G_{2}\times^{G_{0}}\Gamma\right)

\displaystyle\qquad G_{1}\times^{G_{0}}\Gamma

just listed, among others), we will require some notational shorthand to reduce simultaneously the notational clutter but also to expedite the process of weaving through the induction spaces
4
4

4

A secondary benefit of this is that the diagrams which involve the induction spaces, morphisms between them, and their quotients now actually fit within the page margins.
. As such we introduce the following notation for any object

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho_{013}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho_{0,123}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho_{123}\times^{G_{0}}\id_{\Gamma}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Gamma_{023}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Gamma_{0123}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Gamma_{03}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho_{023}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho_{012,3}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\id_{G_{3}}\times^{G_{2}}\rho_{012}}

Upon taking
G_{3}
-quotients, we see that these maps coincide by the universal property of the quotient object and hence give rise to a commuting diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\,{}_{\Gamma_{013}}{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\,{}_{\Gamma_{0123}}{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\,{}_{\Gamma_{03}}{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\,{}_{\Gamma_{023}}{X}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\rho}_{013}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\id_{G_{3}}\times^{G_{2}}\rho_{012}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\rho_{123}\times^{G_{0}}\Gamma}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\rho}_{0,123}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\rho}_{012,3}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\rho}_{023}}

Our next two lemmas show that all these morphisms induce the same isomorphisms
\XGamma\xrightarrow{\cong}\,_{\Gamma_{0123}}{X}
and
h_{\Gamma}^{03}:\XGamma\to\,_{\Gamma_{03}}{X}
. In this sense, they are “cancellation associative” and provide a way of weaving between perspectives on our quotients.

Lemma 7.2.11
.

Let
G_{0}\xrightarrow{\varphi}G_{1}\xrightarrow{\varphi}G_{2}\xrightarrow{\rho}G_{3}
be morphisms of group objects and let
X
be a left
G_{2}
-object. Then the natural isomorphisms
h_{\Gamma}^{01},

h_{\Gamma}^{02},

h_{\Gamma_{01}}^{13}
, and
h_{\Gamma_{02}}^{23}
are cancellation associative in the sense that for every smooth free
G_{0}
-variety
\Gamma
, the diagrams

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\,{}_{\Gamma_{013}}{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\,{}_{\Gamma_{0123}}{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\,{}_{\Gamma_{01}}{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\XGamma}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left(\overline{\rho_{123}\times^{G_{0}}\id_{\Gamma}}\right)^{-1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h_{\Gamma_{01}}^{13}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h_{\Gamma}^{01}}

and

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\,{}_{\Gamma_{023}}{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\,{}_{\Gamma_{0123}}{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\,{}_{\Gamma_{02}}{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\XGamma}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left(\overline{\id_{G_{3}}\times^{G_{2}}\rho_{012}}\right)^{-1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h_{\Gamma_{02}}^{23}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h_{\Gamma}^{02}}

both commute and coincide.

Proof.

This is routine by using the universal property of the quotients which appear in the diagrams above.
∎

Because of the lack of ambiguity, we define the map
h_{\Gamma}^{0123}:\XGamma\to\,_{\Gamma_{0123}}{X}
by

(7.2.3)

h_{\Gamma}^{0123}:=\left(\overline{\rho_{123}\times^{G_{0}}\id_{\Gamma}}\right)^{-1}\circ h_{\Gamma_{01}}^{13}\circ h_{\Gamma}^{01}.

Lemma 7.2.12
.

Let
\Gamma
be a smooth free
G_{0}
-variety, let
\hat{\Gamma}
be a smooth free
G_{1}
-variety, and let
X
be a
G_{3}
-variety. Then the diagrams

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\,{}_{\hat{\Gamma}}{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\,{}_{\hat{\Gamma}_{12}}{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\,{}_{\hat{\Gamma}_{13}}{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\,{}_{\hat{\Gamma}_{123}}{X}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h_{\hat{\Gamma}}^{13}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h_{\hat{\Gamma}}^{12}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h^{23}_{\hat{\Gamma}_{12}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\rho}_{123}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\XGamma}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\,{}_{\Gamma_{01}}{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\,{}_{\Gamma_{03}}{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\,{}_{\Gamma_{013}}{X}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h_{\Gamma}^{01}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h_{\Gamma}^{03}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h_{\Gamma_{01}}^{13}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\rho}_{013}}

and

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\XGamma}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\,{}_{\Gamma_{02}}{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\,{}_{\Gamma_{03}}{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\,{}_{\Gamma_{023}}{X}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h_{\Gamma}^{02}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h_{\Gamma}^{03}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h_{\Gamma_{02}}^{23}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\rho}_{023}}

all commute.

Proof.

This is a routine proof using the universal property of each quotient variety in sight.
∎

Combining these lemmas together allows us to produce the commuting cube:

(7.2.4)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\,{}_{\Gamma_{02}}{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\,{}_{\Gamma_{023}}{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\XGamma}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\,{}_{\Gamma_{03}}{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\,{}_{\Gamma_{012}}{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\,{}_{\Gamma_{0123}}{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\,{}_{\Gamma_{01}}{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\,{}_{\Gamma_{013}}{X}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h_{\Gamma_{02}}^{23}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\rho}_{012}^{-1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left(\overline{\id_{G_{3}}\times^{G_{2}}\rho_{012}}\right)^{-1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h_{\Gamma}^{02}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h_{\Gamma}^{01}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\rho}_{023}^{-1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h_{\Gamma_{012}}^{23}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h_{\Gamma_{01}}^{13}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h_{\Gamma_{01}}^{12}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left(\overline{\rho_{123}\times^{G_{0}}\id_{\Gamma}}\right)^{-1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h_{\Gamma}^{03}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\rho}_{013}^{-1}}

An immediate consequence of the lemmas above, the commuting cube, and the definition of
h_{\Gamma}^{0123}
are the following myriad alternate factorizations of
h_{\Gamma}^{0123}
.

Lemma 7.2.13
.

For any left
G_{3}
-object
X
and any smooth free
G_{0}
-variety (respectively free
G_{0}
-space)
\Gamma
we have the following factorizations of the morphism
h_{\Gamma}^{0123}
:

\displaystyle h_{\Gamma}^{0123}

\displaystyle=\left(\overline{\rho_{123}\times^{G_{0}}\id_{\Gamma}}\right)^{-1}\circ h_{\Gamma_{01}}^{13}\circ h_{\Gamma}^{01}=\left(\overline{\id_{G_{3}}\times^{G_{2}}\rho_{123}}\right)^{-1}\circ h_{\Gamma_{02}}^{23}\circ h_{\Gamma}^{02}

\displaystyle=h^{23}_{\Gamma_{012}}\circ\left(\overline{\rho}_{012}\right)^{-1}\circ h_{\Gamma}^{02}=h_{\Gamma_{012}}^{23}\circ h_{\Gamma_{01}}\circ h_{\Gamma}^{01}

\displaystyle=\left(\overline{\rho_{123}\times^{G_{0}}\id_{\Gamma}}\right)^{-1}\circ\left(\overline{\rho}_{013}\right)^{-1}\circ h_{\Gamma}^{03}=\left(\overline{\id_{G_{3}}\times^{G_{2}}\rho_{123}}\right)^{-1}\circ\left(\overline{\rho}_{023}\right)^{-1}\circ h_{\Gamma}^{03}.

Proof.

These different factorizations are simply the total paths starting at
\XGamma
and ending at
\,{}_{\Gamma_{0123}}{X}
in the cube presented in Diagram
7.2.4
.
∎

Returning explicitly to the task of verifying the (pseudofunctorial) associativity of the Change of Groups functors, we recall that we must show that the pasting diagram presented in Diagram
7.2.1
is equal to the pasting diagram presented in Diagram
7.2.2
. However, the first pasting diagram is ultimately given as the composite

\left(\varphi_{01}^{\sharp}\ast\alpha_{123}\right)\circ\alpha_{0,123}:(\varphi_{23}\circ\varphi_{12}\circ\varphi_{01})^{\sharp}\Rightarrow\left(\varphi_{01}^{\sharp}\circ(\varphi_{23}\circ\varphi_{12})^{\sharp}\right)\Rightarrow\varphi_{01}^{\sharp}\circ\varphi_{12}^{\sharp}\circ\varphi_{23}^{\sharp}

while the second is given by

\left(\alpha_{012}\ast\varphi_{23}^{\sharp}\right)\circ\alpha_{012,3}:(\varphi_{23}\circ\varphi_{12}\circ\varphi_{01})^{\sharp}\Rightarrow\left((\varphi_{12}\circ\varphi_{01})^{\sharp}\circ\varphi_{23}^{\sharp}\right)\Rightarrow\varphi_{01}^{\sharp}\circ\varphi_{12}^{\sharp}\circ\varphi_{23}^{\sharp}.

It thus suffices to prove that

(7.2.5)

(\varphi_{01}^{\sharp}\ast\alpha_{123})\circ\alpha_{0,123}=(\alpha_{012}\ast\varphi_{23}^{\sharp})\circ\alpha_{012,3}

in order to conclude that the Change of Groups functors vary pseudofunctorially over the category of smooth algebraic groups. To this end we compute using the proof of Proposition
7.1.18
that on one hand for any object
\Gamma
in
\Sf(G_{0})
(respectively any object
\Gamma
in
\mathbf{Free}(G_{0})
)

\,{}_{\Gamma}{\alpha_{0,123}}

\displaystyle=\overline{F}\left(h_{\Gamma}^{01}\right)\left(\overline{F}\left(h_{\Gamma_{01}}^{13}\right)\left(\tau_{\overline{\rho}_{013}}^{A}\right)\right)\circ\,_{\Gamma}{\left(\phi_{h^{01},h^{13}}\ast\overline{F}(\overline{\rho}_{013})\right)^{-1}}\circ\,_{\Gamma}{\left(\phi_{h^{13}\circ h^{01},\overline{\rho}_{013}}\right)^{-1}},

while on the other hand

\,{}_{\Gamma}{\alpha_{012,3}}

\displaystyle=\overline{F}\left(h_{\Gamma}^{02}\right)\left(\overline{F}\left(h^{23}_{\Gamma_{02}}\right)\left(\tau_{\overline{\rho}_{023}}^{A}\right)\right)\circ\,_{\Gamma}{\left(\phi_{h^{02},h^{23}}\ast\overline{F}(\overline{\rho}_{023})\right)^{-1}}\circ\,_{\Gamma}{\left(\phi_{h^{23}\circ h^{02},\rho_{023}}\right)^{-1}}.

Computing also using that
\overline{F}
is a pseudofunctor gives that

\,{}_{\Gamma}{\left(\phi_{h^{01},h^{13}}\ast\overline{F}(\overline{\rho}_{013})\right)^{-1}}\circ\,_{\Gamma}{\left(\phi_{h^{13}\circ h^{01},\overline{\rho}_{013}}\right)^{-1}}

\displaystyle=\,_{\Gamma}{\left(\phi_{h^{01}\circ h^{13},\rho_{013}}\circ\left(\phi_{h^{01},h^{13}}\ast\overline{F}(\overline{\rho}_{013})\right)\right)^{-1}}

\displaystyle=\,_{\Gamma}{\left(\phi_{h^{01},\overline{\rho}_{013}\circ h^{13}}\circ\left(\overline{F}\left(h^{01}_{\Gamma}\right)\ast\phi_{h^{13},\rho_{013}}\right)\right)^{-1}}

\displaystyle=\,_{\Gamma}{\left(\overline{F}\left(h^{01}_{\Gamma}\right)\ast\phi_{h^{13},\rho_{013}}\right)^{-1}}\circ\,_{\Gamma}{\left(\phi_{h^{01},\overline{\rho}_{013}\circ h^{13}}\right)^{-1}}

and similarly

\,{}_{\Gamma}{\left(\phi_{h^{02},h^{23}}\ast\overline{F}(\overline{\rho}_{023})\right)^{-1}}\circ\,_{\Gamma}{\left(\phi_{h^{23}\circ h^{02},\overline{\rho}_{023}}\right)^{-1}}

\displaystyle=\,_{\Gamma}{\left(\phi_{h^{02}\circ h^{23},\rho_{023}}\circ\left(\phi_{h^{02},h^{23}}\ast\overline{F}(\overline{\rho}_{023})\right)\right)^{-1}}

\displaystyle=\,_{\Gamma}{\left(\phi_{h^{02},\overline{\rho}_{023}\circ h^{23}}\circ\left(\overline{F}\left(h^{02}_{\Gamma}\right)\ast\phi_{h^{23},\rho_{023}}\right)\right)^{-1}}

\displaystyle=\,_{\Gamma}{\left(\overline{F}\left(h^{02}_{\Gamma}\right)\ast\phi_{h^{23},\rho_{023}}\right)^{-1}}\circ\,_{\Gamma}{\left(\phi_{h^{02},\overline{\rho}_{023}\circ h^{23}}\right)^{-1}}.

Putting these observations together gives the calculation

\,{}_{\Gamma}{\alpha_{0,123}}

\displaystyle=\overline{F}\left(h_{\Gamma}^{01}\right)\left(\overline{F}\left(h_{\Gamma_{01}}^{13}\right)\left(\tau_{\overline{\rho}_{013}}^{A}\right)\right)\circ\,_{\Gamma}{\left(\overline{F}\left(h^{01}_{\Gamma}\right)\ast\phi_{h^{13},\rho_{013}}\right)^{-1}}\circ\,_{\Gamma}{\left(\phi_{h^{01},\overline{\rho}_{013}\circ h^{13}}\right)^{-1}}

\displaystyle=\overline{F}\left(h_{\Gamma}^{01}\right)\left(\overline{F}\left(h_{\Gamma_{01}}^{13}\right)\left(\tau_{\overline{\rho}_{013}}^{A}\right)\circ\left(\phi_{h^{13},\rho_{013}}\right)^{-1}\right)\circ\,_{\Gamma}{\left(\phi_{h^{01},\overline{\rho}_{013}\circ h^{13}}\right)^{-1}}.

and similarly

\,{}_{\Gamma}{\alpha_{012,3}}

\displaystyle=\overline{F}\left(h_{\Gamma}^{02}\right)\left(\overline{F}\left(h_{\Gamma_{02}}^{23}\right)\left(\tau_{\overline{\rho}_{023}}^{A}\right)\circ\left(\phi_{h^{23},\rho_{023}}\right)^{-1}\right)\circ\,_{\Gamma}{\left(\phi_{h^{02},\overline{\rho}_{023}\circ h^{23}}\right)^{-1}}.

To complete the computation of the pasting diagrams we note

\,{}_{\Gamma}{\left(\alpha_{012}\ast\varphi_{23}^{\sharp}\right)}

\displaystyle=\overline{F}\left(h_{\Gamma}^{01}\right)\left(\overline{F}\left(h_{\Gamma_{01}}^{12}\right)\left(\tau_{\overline{\rho}_{012}}^{A}\right)\right)\circ\left(\phi_{h^{01},h^{12}}^{\varphi_{23}^{\sharp}A}\ast\overline{F}(\overline{\rho}_{012})\right)^{-1}\circ\left(\phi_{h^{12}\circ h^{01},\overline{\rho}_{012}}^{\varphi_{23}^{\sharp}A}\right)^{-1}

\displaystyle=\overline{F}\left(h_{\Gamma}^{01}\right)\left(\overline{F}\left(h_{\Gamma_{01}}^{12}\right)\left(\tau_{\overline{\rho}_{012}}^{A}\right)\right)\circ\left(\phi_{h^{12}\circ h^{01},\overline{\rho}_{012}}^{\varphi_{23}^{\sharp}A}\circ\left(\phi_{h^{01},h^{12}}^{\varphi_{23}^{\sharp}A}\ast\overline{F}(\overline{\rho}_{012})\right)\right)^{-1}

\displaystyle=\overline{F}\left(h_{\Gamma}^{01}\right)\left(\overline{F}\left(h_{\Gamma_{01}}^{12}\right)\left(\tau_{\overline{\rho}_{012}}^{A}\right)\right)\circ\left(\phi_{h^{01},\overline{\rho}_{012}\circ h^{12}}^{\varphi_{23}^{\sharp}A}\circ\left(\overline{F}\left(h^{01}_{\Gamma}\right)\ast\phi_{h^{23},\overline{\rho}_{023}}^{\varphi_{23}^{\sharp}A}\right)\right)^{-1}

\displaystyle=\overline{F}\left(h_{\Gamma}^{01}\right)\left(\overline{F}\left(h_{\Gamma_{01}}^{12}\right)\left(\tau_{\overline{\rho}_{012}}^{A}\right)\right)\circ\left(\overline{F}\left(h^{01}_{\Gamma}\right)\ast\phi_{h^{23},\overline{\rho}_{023}}^{\varphi_{23}^{\sharp}A}\right)^{-1}\circ\left(\phi_{h^{01},\overline{\rho}_{012}\circ h^{12}}^{\varphi_{23}^{\sharp}A}\right)^{-1}

\displaystyle=\overline{F}\left(h_{\Gamma}^{01}\right)\left(\overline{F}\left(h_{\Gamma_{01}}^{12}\right)\left(\tau_{\overline{\rho}_{012}}^{A}\right)\circ\phi_{h^{23},\overline{\rho}_{023}}^{\varphi_{23}^{\sharp}A}\right)^{-1}\circ\left(\phi_{h^{01},\overline{\rho}_{012}\circ h^{12}}^{\varphi_{23}^{\sharp}A}\right)^{-1}

and, alternatively,

\,{}_{\Gamma}{\left(\varphi_{01}^{\sharp}\ast\alpha_{123}\right)}=\overline{F}\left(h_{\Gamma}^{01}\right)\left(\,{}_{\Gamma_{01}}{\alpha_{123}}\right)

\displaystyle=\overline{F}\left(h_{\Gamma}^{01}\right)\left(\overline{F}\left(h_{\Gamma_{01}}^{12}\right)\left(\overline{F}\left(h_{\Gamma_{012}}^{23}\right)\left(\tau_{\overline{\rho}_{123}}^{A}\right)\right)\circ\left(\phi_{h^{23}\circ h^{12},\overline{\rho}_{123}}\circ\left(\phi_{h^{12},h^{23}}\ast\overline{F}(\overline{\rho}_{123})\right)\right)^{-1}\right)

\displaystyle=\overline{F}\left(h_{\Gamma}^{01}\right)\left(\overline{F}\left(h_{\Gamma_{01}}^{12}\right)\left(\overline{F}\left(h_{\Gamma_{012}}^{23}\right)\left(\tau_{\overline{\rho}_{123}}^{A}\right)\right)\circ\left(\phi_{h^{23}\circ h^{12},\overline{\rho}_{123}}\circ\left(\overline{F}\left(h_{\Gamma_{01}}^{12}\right)\ast\phi_{h^{23},\overline{\rho}_{123}}\right)\right)^{-1}\right)

\displaystyle=\overline{F}\left(h_{\Gamma}^{01}\right)\left(\overline{F}\left(h_{\Gamma_{01}}^{12}\right)\left(\overline{F}\left(h_{\Gamma_{012}}^{23}\right)\left(\tau_{\overline{\rho}_{123}}^{A}\right)\circ\left(\phi_{h^{23},\overline{\rho}_{123}}\right)^{-1}\right)\circ\left(\phi_{h^{12},\overline{\rho}_{123}\circ h^{23}}\right)^{-1}\right)

Observe that in the last line we used the identities

\displaystyle\left(\phi_{h^{23}\circ h^{12},\overline{\rho}_{123}}\circ\left(\phi_{h^{12},h^{23}}\ast\overline{F}(\overline{\rho}_{123})\right)\right)^{-1}

\displaystyle=\left(\phi_{h^{12},\overline{\rho}_{123}\circ h^{23}}\circ\left(\overline{F}\left(h_{\Gamma_{01}}^{12}\right)\ast\phi_{h^{23},\overline{\rho}_{123}}\right)\right)^{-1}

\displaystyle=\overline{F}\left(h_{\Gamma_{01}}^{12}\right)\left(\phi_{h^{23},\overline{\rho}_{123}}\right)^{-1}\circ\left(\phi_{h^{12},\overline{\rho}_{123}\circ h^{23}}\right)^{-1}.

Combining these above calculations give the lemma below.

Lemma 7.2.14
.

For any smooth free
G_{0}
-variety
\Gamma
(respectively any free
G_{0}
-space
\Gamma
), the equations

\,{}_{\Gamma}{\left(\varphi_{01}^{\sharp}\ast\alpha_{123}\right)}\circ\,_{\Gamma}{\alpha_{0,123}}

\displaystyle=\overline{F}\left(h_{\Gamma}^{01}\right)\left(\overline{F}\left(h_{\Gamma_{01}}^{12}\right)\left(\overline{F}\left(h_{\Gamma_{012}}^{23}\right)\left(\tau_{\overline{\rho}_{123}}^{A}\right)\circ\left(\phi_{h^{23},\overline{\rho}_{123}}\right)^{-1}\right)\circ\left(\phi_{h^{12},\overline{\rho}_{123}\circ h^{23}}\right)^{-1}\right)

\displaystyle\circ\overline{F}\left(h_{\Gamma}^{01}\right)\left(\overline{F}\left(h_{\Gamma_{01}}^{13}\right)\left(\tau_{\overline{\rho}_{013}}^{A}\right)\circ\left(\phi_{h^{13},\rho_{013}}\right)^{-1}\right)\circ\,_{\Gamma}{\left(\phi_{h^{01},\overline{\rho}_{013}\circ h^{13}}\right)^{-1}}

and

\,{}_{\Gamma}{\left(\alpha_{012}\ast\varphi_{23}^{\sharp}\right)}\circ\,_{\Gamma}{\alpha_{012,3}}

\displaystyle=\overline{F}\left(h_{\Gamma}^{01}\right)\left(\overline{F}\left(h_{\Gamma_{01}}^{12}\right)\left(\tau_{\overline{\rho}_{012}}^{A}\right)\circ\phi_{h^{23},\overline{\rho}_{023}}^{\varphi_{23}^{\sharp}A}\right)^{-1}\circ\left(\phi_{h^{01},\overline{\rho}_{012}\circ h^{12}}^{\varphi_{23}^{\sharp}A}\right)^{-1}

\displaystyle\circ\overline{F}\left(h_{\Gamma}^{02}\right)\left(\overline{F}\left(h^{23}_{\Gamma_{02}}\right)\left(\tau_{\overline{\rho}_{023}}^{A}\right)\right)\circ\,_{\Gamma}{\left(\phi_{h^{02},h^{23}}\ast\overline{F}(\overline{\rho}_{023})\right)^{-1}}\circ\,_{\Gamma}{\left(\phi_{h^{23}\circ h^{02},\rho_{023}}\right)^{-1}}

hold.

Theorem 7.2.15
.

The pasting diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{G_{2}}(X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{G_{3}}(X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{G_{1}}(X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{G_{0}}(X)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{12}^{\sharp}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{23}^{\sharp}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\varphi_{23}\circ\varphi_{12}\circ\varphi_{01})^{\sharp}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\varphi_{23}\circ\varphi_{12})^{\sharp}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{01}^{\sharp}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha_{0,123}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha_{123}}

is equal to the pasting diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{G_{2}}(X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{G_{3}}(X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{G_{1}}(X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{G_{0}}(X)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{12}^{\sharp}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\varphi_{12}\circ\varphi_{01})^{\sharp}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{23}^{\sharp}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\varphi_{23}\circ\varphi_{12}\circ\varphi_{01})^{\sharp}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{01}^{\sharp}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha_{012}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha_{012,3}}

coincide.

Proof.

As explained earlier (cf. Equation
7.2.5
), it suffices to prove that the equation

(\varphi_{01}^{\sharp}\ast\alpha_{123})\circ\alpha_{0,123}=(\alpha_{012}\ast\varphi_{23}^{\sharp})\circ\alpha_{012,3}

holds. However, begin by noting that since

\left(\overline{\rho_{123}\times^{G_{0}}\id_{\Gamma}}\right)^{-1}\circ h_{\Gamma_{01}}^{13}\circ h_{\Gamma}^{01}=h_{\Gamma}^{0123}=\left(\overline{\id_{G_{3}}\times^{G_{2}}\rho_{012}}\right)^{-1}\circ h_{\Gamma_{02}}^{23}\circ h_{\Gamma}^{02}

by Lemma
7.2.13
we have

\left(\overline{\id_{G_{3}}\times^{G_{2}}\rho_{012}}\right)\circ\left(\overline{\rho_{123}\times^{G_{0}}\id_{\Gamma}}\right)^{-1}\circ h_{\Gamma_{01}}^{13}\circ h_{\Gamma}^{01}=h_{\Gamma_{02}}^{23}\circ h_{\Gamma}^{02}

and, dually,

h_{\Gamma_{01}}^{13}\circ h_{\Gamma}^{01}=\left(\overline{\rho_{123}\times^{G_{0}}\id_{\Gamma}}\right)\circ\left(\overline{\id_{G_{3}}\times^{G_{2}}\rho_{012}}\right)^{-1}\circ h_{\Gamma_{02}}^{23}\circ h_{\Gamma}^{02}

Starting from here we also derive that

\displaystyle\left(\overline{\rho_{123}\times^{G_{0}}\id_{\Gamma}}\right)\circ\left(\overline{\id_{G_{3}}\times^{G_{2}}\rho_{012}}\right)^{-1}\circ h_{\Gamma_{02}}^{23}\circ h_{\Gamma}^{02}

\displaystyle=\left(\overline{\rho_{123}\times^{G_{0}}\id_{\Gamma}}\right)\circ h_{\Gamma_{012}}^{23}\circ\left(\overline{\rho}_{012}\right)^{-1}\circ h_{\Gamma}^{02}

\displaystyle=\left(\overline{\rho_{123}\times^{G_{0}}\id_{\Gamma}}\right)\circ h_{\Gamma_{012}}^{23}\circ h_{\Gamma_{01}}^{12}\circ h_{\Gamma}^{01}.

Using the psuedofunctoriality of
\overline{F}
, the various rewritings

\phi_{b\circ a,c}\circ(\phi_{a,b}\ast\overline{F}(c))=\phi_{a,c\circ b}\circ(\overline{F}(a)\ast\phi_{b,c})

of the compositors when necessary, myriad identities implied by Diagram
7.2.4
, and performing an extremely tedious algebraic chase allows us to derive that

\displaystyle\overline{F}\left(h_{\Gamma}^{01}\right)\left(\overline{F}\left(h_{\Gamma_{01}}^{12}\right)\left(\overline{F}\left(h_{\Gamma_{012}}^{23}\right)\left(\tau_{\overline{\rho}_{123}}^{A}\right)\circ\left(\phi_{h^{23},\overline{\rho}_{123}}\right)^{-1}\right)\circ\left(\phi_{h^{12},\overline{\rho}_{123}\circ h^{23}}\right)^{-1}\right)

\displaystyle\circ\overline{F}\left(h_{\Gamma}^{01}\right)\left(\overline{F}\left(h_{\Gamma_{01}}^{13}\right)\left(\tau_{\overline{\rho}_{013}}^{A}\right)\circ\left(\phi_{h^{13},\rho_{013}}\right)^{-1}\right)\circ\,_{\Gamma}{\left(\phi_{h^{01},\overline{\rho}_{013}\circ h^{13}}\right)^{-1}}

\displaystyle=\overline{F}\left(h_{\Gamma}^{01}\right)\left(\overline{F}\left(h_{\Gamma_{01}}^{12}\right)\left(\tau_{\overline{\rho}_{012}}^{A}\right)\circ\phi_{h^{23},\overline{\rho}_{023}}^{\varphi_{23}^{\sharp}A}\right)^{-1}\circ\left(\phi_{h^{01},\overline{\rho}_{012}\circ h^{12}}^{\varphi_{23}^{\sharp}A}\right)^{-1}

\displaystyle\circ\overline{F}\left(h_{\Gamma}^{02}\right)\left(\overline{F}\left(h^{23}_{\Gamma_{02}}\right)\left(\tau_{\overline{\rho}_{023}}^{A}\right)\right)\circ\,_{\Gamma}{\left(\phi_{h^{02},h^{23}}\ast\overline{F}(\overline{\rho}_{023})\right)^{-1}}\circ\,_{\Gamma}{\left(\phi_{h^{23}\circ h^{02},\rho_{023}}\right)^{-1}}.

However, this exactly allows us to deduce the identity

(\varphi_{01}^{\sharp}\ast\alpha_{123})\circ\alpha_{0,123}=(\alpha_{012}\ast\varphi_{23}^{\sharp})\circ\alpha_{012,3},

as was desired.
∎

As an immediate corollary to this theorem we can regard the Change of Groups functors as the fibre functors for a pseudofunctor with domain
\mathbf{SAlgGrp}\downarrow H
in the geometric case and
\mathbf{TopGrp}\downarrow H
in the topological case.

Corollary 7.2.16
.

Let
H
be a smooth algebraic group (respectively a topological group), let
X
be a left
H
-variety (respectively a left
H
-space), and let
(F,\overline{F})
be an
H
-pre-equivariant psuedofunctor on
X
. Then there is a pseudofunctor

F_{-}(X):\left(\mathbf{SAlgGrp}\downarrow H\right)^{\op}\to\fCat

where:

•

For each smooth algebraic group morphism
\varphi:G\to H
,
F_{-}(X)(\varphi):=F_{G}(X)
for the
(G,H)
-pre-equivariant pseudofunctor

\left(\left(\overline{F}\left(G\backslash(H\times^{G}((-)\times X))\right),\overline{F}\right),(F,\overline{F}),\id_{\overline{F}}\right);

•

For each morphism
\varphi:G\to G^{\prime}
in
\mathbf{SAlgGrp}\downarrow H
, the fibre functor is given by the Change of Groups functor

\varphi^{\sharp}:F_{G^{\prime}}(X)\to F_{G}(X).

•

For each composable pair of morphisms
\varphi_{01}:G_{0}\to G_{1}
and
\varphi_{12}:G_{1}\to G_{2}
in
\mathbf{SAlgGrp}\downarrow H
, the compositor
\phi_{\varphi_{01},\varphi_{12}}
is defined by

\phi_{\varphi_{01},\varphi_{12}}:=\alpha_{012}^{-1},

where
\alpha_{012}
is the natural isomorphism of Proposition
7.1.18
.

Respectively, there is also a pseudofunctor
F_{-}(X):\left(\mathbf{TopGrp}\downarrow H\right)^{\op}\to\fCat
whose assignment is given similarly.

Proof.

The only difficult aspect of this corollary lies in verifying that the compositors vary pseudofunctorially in the category
(\mathbf{SAlgGrp}\downarrow H)^{\op}
for the geometric case and in the category
(\mathbf{TopGrp}\downarrow H)^{\op}
in the topological case; however, this is exactly the content of Theorem
7.2.15
.
∎

This allows us to formulate two different perspectives on Change of Groups: first a more general version valid for any
(G,H)
-pre-equivariant pseudofunctor and second a perspective given in terms of the
2
-category
({-},{H})\mathfrak{PreEq}(\mathnormal{X})
.

In the first case assume that we are in either of the situations below:

(1)

smooth algebraic groups
G
and
H
, a group morphism
\varphi:G\to H
, and also a left
H
-variety
X
;

(2)

topological groups
G
and
H
, a group morphism
\varphi:G\to H
, and also a left
H
-space
X
;

Now consider a
(G,H)
-pre-equivariant pseudofunctor
\left((F,\overline{F}),(E,\overline{E}),e:\overline{F}\Rightarrow\overline{E}\right).
We then have two distinct ways to define the Change of Groups functors
\varphi^{\sharp}:F_{H}(X)\to E_{G}(X)
: the first is via the composition

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{H}(X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{G}(X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\PC(\overline{F}\circ\quo_{G}^{\op})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E_{G}(X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\PC(\overline{E}\circ\quo_{G}^{\op})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\,{}_{F}{\varphi^{\sharp}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\PC(e\ast\quo_{G}^{\op})}

while the second is via

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{H}(X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\PC(\overline{F}\circ\quo_{H}^{\op})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\PC(\overline{E}\circ\quo_{H}^{\op})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E_{G}(X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\PC(\overline{E}\circ\quo_{G}^{\op})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\PC(e\ast\quo_{H}^{\op})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\,{}_{E}{\varphi^{\sharp}}}

where we use the left-handed subscripts
\,{}_{F}{\varphi^{\sharp}}
and
\,{}_{E}{\varphi^{\sharp}}
to denote the pseudofunctor with which we change groups. The first composite acts on objects
A
by defining, for each
\Gamma\in\Sf(G)_{0}
(respectively for each
\Gamma\in\mathbf{Free}(G)_{0}
),

\,{}_{\Gamma}{\left(\PC(e\ast\quo_{G}^{\op})\circ\,_{F}{\varphi^{\sharp}}\right)(A)}=\,_{\Gamma}{e}\left(\overline{F}\left(h_{\Gamma}\right)\left(\,{}_{H\times^{G}\Gamma}{A}\right)\right)

while the second acts by setting

\,{}_{\Gamma}{\left(\,{}_{E}{\varphi^{\sharp}}\circ\PC(e\ast\quo_{H}^{\op})\right)}=\overline{E}\left(h_{\Gamma}\right)\left(\,{}_{\Gamma}{e}\left(\,{}_{H\times^{G}\Gamma}{A}\right)\right).

These objects are then equal by the pseudofunctoriality of the Change of Groups functors together with the fact that by definition
e
is a pseudonatural equivalence from
\overline{F}
to
\overline{E}
. In particular, for a general
(G,H)
-pre-equivariant pseudofunctor we get an unambiguous definition for our Change of Groups functors.

Lemma 7.2.17
.

Assume we are in either of the cases below.

(1)

smooth algebraic groups
G
and
H
, a group morphism
\varphi:G\to H
, and also a left
H
-variety
X
;

(2)

topological groups
G
and
H
, a group morphism
\varphi:G\to H
, and also a left
H
-space
X
.

If
\left((F,\overline{F}),(E,\overline{E}),e:\overline{F}\Rightarrow\overline{E}\right)
is a
(G,H)
-pre-equivariant pseudofunctor then

\PC(e\ast\quo_{G}^{\op})\circ\,_{F}{\varphi^{\sharp}}=\,_{E}{\varphi^{\sharp}}\circ\PC(e\ast\quo_{H}^{\op}).

Definition 7.2.18
.

Assume we are in either of the cases below.

(1)

smooth algebraic groups
G
and
H
, a group morphism
\varphi:G\to H
, and also a left
H
-variety
X
;

(2)

topological groups
G
and
H
, a group morphism
\varphi:G\to H
, and also a left
H
-space
X
.

Then for any
(G,H)
-pre-equivariant pseudofunctor
\left((F,\overline{F}),(E,\overline{E}),e:\overline{F}\Rightarrow\overline{E}\right)
on
X
, the Change of Groups functor

\varphi^{\sharp}:F_{H}(X)\to E_{G}(X)

is given by
\varphi^{\sharp}:=\PC(e\ast\quo_{G}^{\op})\circ\,_{F}{\varphi^{\sharp}}.

Using this we can now define our primary four
2
-functors
({-},{H})\mathfrak{PreEq}(\mathnormal{X})\to\fCat
. Define the functors as follows:

•

The functor
\PC_{1}:({-},{H})\mathfrak{PreEq}(\mathnormal{X})\to\fCat
is induced by the assignment

\left((F,\overline{F}),(E,\overline{E}),e\right)\mapsto\PC(\overline{F})

on objects.

•

The functor
\PC_{2}:({-},{H})\mathfrak{PreEq}(\mathnormal{X})\to\fCat
is induced by the assignment

\left((F,\overline{F}),(E,\overline{E}),e\right)\mapsto\PC(\overline{E})

on objects.

•

The functor
(-)_{H}(X):({-},{H})\mathfrak{PreEq}(\mathnormal{X})\to\fCat
is induced by the assignment

\left((F,\overline{F}),(E,\overline{E}),e\right)\mapsto F_{H}(X)

on objects.

•

The functor
(-)_{\square}(X)\to\fCat
is induced by the assignment

\left((F,\overline{F}),(E,\overline{E}),e\right)\mapsto E_{G}(X)

where
G
is the group for which the object quintuple is a
(G,H)
-pre-equivariant pseudofunctor.

By construction there is a (pseudo)natural equivalence of
2
-functors

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces({-},{H})\mathfrak{PreEq}(\mathnormal{X})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\fCat}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\PC_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\PC_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

which is induced by
e
. Our high-level perspective on the Change of Groups functors is that they give rise to a pseudonatural transformation

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces({-},{H})\mathfrak{PreEq}(\mathnormal{X})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\fCat}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-)_{H}(X)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-)_{\square}(X)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-)^{\sharp}}

which we describe in the next proposition.

Proposition 7.2.19
.

Assume we are in either of the cases below.

(1)

smooth algebraic groups
G
and
H
, a group morphism
\varphi:G\to H
, and also a left
H
-variety
X
;

(2)

topological groups
G
and
H
, a group morphism
\varphi:G\to H
, and also a left
H
-space
X
.

There is then a pseudonatural transformation

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces({-},{H})\mathfrak{PreEq}(\mathnormal{X})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\fCat}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-)_{H}(X)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-)_{\square}(X)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-)^{\sharp}}

induced by declaring the object functors
\,{}_{\underline{{F}}}{(-)^{\sharp}}
for a
(G,H)
-pre-equivariant pseudofunctor

\underline{{F}}=\left((F,\overline{F}),(E,\overline{E}),e:\overline{F}\xRightarrow{\simeq}\overline{E}\right)

to be given by the Change of Groups functor
\varphi^{\sharp}:F_{H}(X)\to E_{G}(X)
.

Proof.

The witness natural isomorphisms and transformations are induced by the various pseudofunctor witness isomorphisms associated to the Change of Groups. That this determines a pseudonatural transformation is a straightforward and routine verification and omitted.
∎

7.3.
The Induction and Quotient Equivalences

We now present a short section giving sanity check results by extending what are known as the induction and quotient equivalences to our pseudocone formalism in both the topological and geometric cases. It has been known since
[
6
]
in the topological case that for any topological group
G
with topological subgroup
H\leq G
, for any
G
-space
X
there are equivalences of categories

D_{H}^{b}(X)\simeq D_{G}^{b}\left(G\times^{H}X\right)

and, whenever
X
is a free
G
-space,

D_{G}^{b}(X)\simeq D_{G/H}^{b}\left(H\backslash X\right)

which are called the induction and quotient equivalences, respectively. The proofs of these results in
[
6
]
are given entirely in terms of the resolution categories (cf. Lemmas
7.3.1
and
7.3.2
), and so carry over to the pseudocone language freely; consequently, our main goal is to derive these for the variety-theoretic case and also give suggestions for how to further generalize these results to other situations.

Lemma 7.3.1

(
[
6
]
)
.

Let
G
be a topological group and let
H\trianglelefteq G
be a normal topological subgroup with
X
a free
G
-space, there is an equivalence of categories

\Resl_{G}(X)\simeq\Resl_{G/H}(H\backslash X).

Sketch.

This is argued in the proof of the unnumbered theorem in
[
6
, Section 2.6.2]
.
∎

Lemma 7.3.2

(
[
6
]
)
.

Let
H
be a topological subgroup of a topological group
G
with inclusion
i:H\to G
and let
X
be a left
G
-space. Then there is an equivalence of categories

\Resl_{G}\left(G\times^{H}X\right)\simeq\Resl_{H}(X).

Sketch.

This is sketched in the proof of the unnumbered theorem in
[
6
, Section 2.6.3]
.
∎

We will give the geometric analogues of these Lemmas below and illustrate more-or-less how they are proved; for the moment we simply need to know that they hold. From these lemmas we get the propositions below that give the topological versions of the quotient and induction equivalences.

Proposition 7.3.3
.

Let
G
be a topological group,
X
a free
G
-space, and
H\trianglelefteq G
a normal topological subgroup with inclusion
i:H\to G
. Then for any pseudofunctor
\overline{F}:\Top^{\op}\to\fCat
there is an equivalence of categories

F_{G}(X)\simeq F_{G/H}(H\backslash X)

for the induced pre-equivariant pseudofunctors on
X
and
H\backslash X
, respectively.

Proof.

Because of the pseudofunctor
\overline{F}:\Top^{\op}\to\fCat
, we get corresponding induced pre-equivariant pseudofunctors
(\overline{F}\circ\quo_{G}^{\op},\overline{F})
and
(\overline{F}\circ\quo_{G/H}^{\op})
on
X
and
H\backslash X
, respectively. Then if we write

e:\Resl_{G}(X)\xrightarrow{\simeq}\Resl_{G/H}(H\backslash X)

for one direction of the equivalence of Lemma
7.3.1
, we get that the functor

\PC(\overline{F}\ast e):\PC(\overline{F}\circ\quo_{G}^{\op})\xrightarrow{\simeq}\PC(\overline{F}\circ\quo_{G/H}^{\op})

is an equivalence. Because
\PC(\overline{F}\circ\quo_{G}^{\op})=F_{G}(X)
and
\PC(\overline{F}\ast\quo_{G/H}^{\op})=F_{G/H}(H\backslash X)
, the result follows.
∎

Proposition 7.3.4
.

Let
G
be a topological group,
X
a left
G
-space, and
H\leq G
a topological subgroup with inclusion
i:H\to G
. Then for any pseudofunctor
\overline{F}:\Top^{\op}\to\fCat
there is an equivalence of categories

F_{H}(X)\simeq F_{G}\left(G\times^{H}X\right)

for the induced pre-equivariant pseudofunctors on
X
and
G\times^{H}X
, respectively.

Proof.

Because of the pseudofunctor
\overline{F}:\Top^{\op}\to\fCat
, we get corresponding induced pre-equivariant pseudofunctors
(\overline{F}\circ\quo_{H}^{\op},\overline{F})
and
(\overline{F}\circ\quo_{G}^{\op})
on
X
and
G\times^{H}X
, respectively. Then if we write

e:\Resl_{H}(X)\xrightarrow{\simeq}\Resl_{G}\left(G\times^{H}X\right)

for one direction of the equivalence of Lemma
7.3.2
, we get that the functor

\PC(\overline{F}\ast e):\PC(\overline{F}\circ\quo_{H}^{\op})\xrightarrow{\simeq}\PC(\overline{F}\circ\quo_{G}^{\op})

is an equivalence. Because
\PC(\overline{F}\circ\quo_{G}^{\op})=F_{G}(X)
and
\PC(\overline{F}\ast\quo_{G/H}^{\op})=F_{G/H}(H\backslash X)
, the result follows.
∎

We now prove the geometric analogues of the results above by following the same basic techniques as before: providing equivalences between resolution categories. Our first requirement is to show the induction resolution equivalences. Fix a smooth algebraic group
G
with a left
G
-variety
X
and with an algebraic subgroup
i:H\to G
. Upon restricting the
G
-action on
X
to an
H
-action, we obtain a morphism
X\to G\times^{H}X
given by
x\mapsto[1_{G},x]_{H}
on points.

Lemma 7.3.5
.

Let
G
be a smooth algebraic group, let
X
be a left
G
-variety, and let
I:H\to G
be a smooth algebraic subgroup. Then there is an equivalence of categories

\Resl_{H}(X)\simeq\Resl_{G}\left(G\times^{H}X\right).

Proof.

Because
H
is a group subvariety of
G
, we find that the induced functor
\Resl_{H}(X)\to\Resl_{G}(G\times^{H}X)
given by
P\mapsto G\times^{H}P
is fully faithful. To see essential surjectivity, let
Q\to G\times^{H}X
be a locally isotrivial
G
-fibration and write the corresponding quotient maps as
q:Q\to G\backslash Q
. Because
Q
is a locally isotrivial
G
-fibration, we can find a cover
\{\varphi_{i}:U_{i}\to G\backslash(G\times^{H}X)\;|\;i\in I\}
of finite étale opens for which there are commuting diagrams

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q^{-1}(U_{i})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G\times U_{i}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U_{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{2}}

for each
i\in I
. But now observe that
G\backslash Q\cong H\backslash i^{\ast}Q
where
i^{\ast}Q
takes the variety
Q
and regards it as an
H
-variety via the restriction action. Then the composition

U_{i}\xrightarrow{\varphi_{i}}G\backslash Q\xrightarrow{\cong}H\backslash i^{\ast}Q

is a finite étale open and the maps
\{U_{i}\to H\backslash i^{\ast}Q\;|\;i\in I\}
constitute a cover by virtue of the facts that covers compose and isomorphisms are covers. Write
q^{\prime}:i^{\ast}Q\to H\backslash i^{\ast}Q
for the quotient map. It is then routine to check that in the pullback diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(q^{\prime})^{-1}(U_{i})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i^{\ast}Q}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U_{i}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H\backslash i^{\ast}Q}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q^{\prime}}

we have an isomorphism
(q^{\prime})^{-1}(U_{i})\cong H\times U_{i}
which fits into the diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(q^{\prime})^{-1}(U_{i})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H\times U_{i}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U_{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{2}}

It then follows that
i^{\ast}Q
is a locally isotrival
H
-variety. Moreover, by construction, we also have that the map
Q\to G\times^{H}i^{\ast}Q
is an isomorphism
G\times^{H}i^{\ast}Q\cong Q
given by
q\mapsto[1_{G},q]_{H}
. Thus the functor
\Resl_{H}(X)\to\Resl_{G}(G\times^{H}X)
given by
P\mapsto G\times^{H}X
is essentially surjective and hence an equivalence.
∎

Proposition 7.3.6
.

Let
G
be a smooth algebraic group,
X
a left
G
-variety, and
H\leq G
a smooth algebraic subgroup with inclusion
i:H\to G
. Then for any pseudofunctor
\overline{F}:\Var_{/K}^{\op}\to\fCat
there is an equivalence of categories

F_{H}(X)\simeq F_{G}\left(G\times^{H}X\right)

for the induced pre-equivariant pseudofunctors on
X
and
G\times^{H}X
, respectively.

Proof.

This follows mutatis mutandis to the proof of Proposition
7.3.4
by replacing the use of Lemma
7.3.2
with Lemma
7.3.5
instead.
∎

We now show the geometric analogue of the quotient equivalence. This is slightly more involved than the induction case, as we will need

Lemma 7.3.7
.

Let
G
be a smooth algebraic group,
H
a smooth algebraic normal subgroup of
G
with inclusion
i:H\to G
, let
X
be a free
G
-variety, and let
P\to X
be a smooth free
G
-resolution. Then
H\backslash P
is a smooth free
G/H
-resolution of
H\backslash X
. In particular there is a functor
\Resl_{G}(X)\to\Resl_{G\backslash H}(H\backslash X)
given by
P\mapsto H\backslash P
on objects.

Proof.

The existence of the quotients
H\backslash P
and
H\backslash X
follow from the fact that
G
is a smooth free
H
-variety. Now proving that
H\backslash P
is a smooth free
(G/H)
-space follows from the isomorphism

G\backslash P\cong\left(G\backslash H\right)\backslash\left(H\backslash P\right)

and techniques used in the proof of Lemma
7.3.5
by passing a trivialization
U_{i}\to G\backslash P
through the isomorphism above give the desired local isotrivalization. The statement regarding functoriality is also immediate.
∎

We now need a geometric analogue of
[
6
, Lemma 2.1.1]
which says that if
X
is a free
G
-space then the only equivariant morphisms
P\to X
are necessarily
G
-resolutions and satisfy the isomorphism

P\cong X\times_{G\backslash X}G\backslash P.

This isomorphism is necessary to build the other half of our equivalence of categories
\Resl_{G/H}(H\backslash X)\to\Resl_{G}(X)
.

Lemma 7.3.8

(
[
6
, Lemma 2.1.1]
)
.

Let
G
be a smooth algebraic group,
X
a locally isotrivial
G
-space, and let
P\to X
be a
G
-resolution. Then there is an isomorphism of
G
-varieties

P\cong X\times_{G\backslash X}(G\backslash P)

Sketch.

We geometrize the proof of
[
6
, Lemma 2.1.1]
, which is only given for topological spaces. Because
P\to X
is a
G
-resolution, by construction we have that there is unique map
\overline{p}
making the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G\backslash P}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G\backslash X}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\quo_{P}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\quo_{X}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{p}}

commute so there is a natural morphism
\theta:X\times_{G\backslash X}G\backslash P
of cones over the diagram
X\to G\backslash X\leftarrow G\backslash P
. Because every quotient in sight is a geometric quotient and
\quo_{X}:X\to G\backslash X
is locally isotrivial, in order to prove that
P
is isomorphic to the claimed pullback it suffices to work over a finite étale trivialization of the quotient
\quo_{X}:X\to G\backslash X
.

With this in mind let
\{U_{i}\to G\backslash X\;|\;i\in I\}
be such a trivialization and fix some
U=U_{i}
. Define the finite étale open
V\to G\backslash P
via the pullback:
